Baryon-meson scattering amplitude in the 1 / N c 1/N_{c} expansion
Víctor Miguel Banda Guzmán, Rubén Flores-Mendieta, Johann Hernández

Abstract
The baryon-meson scattering amplitude is computed within the 1 / N c 1/N_{c} expansion of QCD, where N c N_{c} is the number of color charges. The most general expression is obtained by accounting for explicitly the effects of the decuplet-octet baryon mass difference and perturbative flavor S ​ U ​ ( 3 ) SU(3) symmetry breaking. Although the resultant expression is general enough that it can be applied to any incoming and outgoing baryons and mesons, provided that the Gell-Mann–Nishijima scheme is respected, results for N ​ π N\pi scattering processes are explicitly dealt with. With these, some isospin relations are verified to be valid at the physical value N c = 3 N_{c}=3 . The expressions obtained here represent a first effort toward understanding scattering processes in the context of the 1 / N c 1/N_{c} expansion.



I
Introduction

Quantum chromodynamics (QCD) is commonly accepted as the theory of the strong interaction, with quarks and gluons as fundamental fields. QCD is a gauge theory with the local symmetry group
SU(N_{c})
, acting in the internal space of color degrees of freedom, with
N_{c}=3
color charges. The analytical computation of hadron properties from first principles, however, is hampered due to the fact that QCD is strongly coupled at low energies. Two major theories have shed light on the static properties of hadrons. One of them is the large-
N_{c}
limit and the other one is chiral perturbation theory (ChPT).

On the one hand, the generalization of QCD from
N_{c}=3
to
N_{c}\to\infty
, usually referred to as large-
N_{c}
QCD, has become a remarkable tool to study the structure and interactions of mesons
tHooft
;
ven
and baryons
witten
in more generality. Physical quantities evaluated in the large-
N_{c}
limit get corrections of relative orders
1/N_{c}
,
1/N_{c}^{2}
, and so on, which originates the
1/N_{c}
expansion of QCD.

On the other hand, chiral symmetry has proved to be quite useful to understand the interactions between mesons and baryons. Calculations beyond current algebra or tree level, however, require an effective low-energy approximation to the underlying and more fundamental QCD gauge theory. Heavy baryon chiral perturbation theory (HBChPT)
jm255
;
jm259
has become since its very formulation a good option for such an effective low-energy approximation. HBChPT has a consistent expansion in powers of momentum and light quark masses because the effective Lagrangian does not contain the baryon mass explicitly. The chiral expansion in powers of the physical quark mass requires the inclusion of both the spin-
1/2
baryon octet and spin-
3/2
baryon decuplet as intermediate states in loop graphs. Jenkins and Manohar first observed
jm259
that chiral logarithmic corrections to the baryon axial vector current in baryon semileptonic decays, including baryon octet and decuplet states in loop graphs, are noticeably smaller than the calculation with intermediate baryon octet states alone. These cancellations can be explained in the context of the
1/N_{c}
expansion: The baryon axial couplings ratios should have
SU(6)
values with
F/D\approx 2/3
, up to corrections of order
1/N_{c}^{2}
for pions
dm1
;
djm94
;
djm95
. Furthermore, loop corrections to the baryon axial vector current with octet and decuplet intermediate states cancel to various orders in
N_{c}
because each individual one-loop diagram is of order
N_{c}
, but the sum of all one-loop diagrams is suppressed by powers of
1/N_{c}

rfm00
. Quite often, a procedure advocated in the literature is to not include intermediate spin-
3/2
baryons explicitly in loops, but to incorporate their effects into the low-energy constants of the effective Lagrangian. This approach could be inconvenient because large numerical cancellations are found when adding contributions of loop diagrams with intermediate baryon octet states and low-energy constants containing the effects of baryon decuplet states.

A further theoretical improvement has been achieved by combining the
1/N_{c}
and chiral expansions into a single formalism which can constrain the low-energy interactions of baryons with the pseudo scalar nonet
\pi
,
K
,
\eta
and
\eta^{\prime}
more effectively than either method separately
jen96
. In a series of publications, some static properties of baryons,
v. gr.
, masses
jen96
, vector current
rfm14
, axial-vector current
rfm21a
, magnetic moments
rfm21b
, etc., have been evaluated in the combined formalism. Two important results were obtained from these analyses. First, large cancellations in the one-loop corrections to these quantities between loop graphs with intermediate spin-1/2 octet and spin-3/2 decuplet baryon states were analytically proved. Second, at the physical value
N_{c}=3
, the combined formalism and HBChPT lead to the same results.

Baryon-meson scattering is a fundamental nuclear physics process which can also be analyzed within the
1/N_{c}
expansion and ChPT. The earliest analysis on the subject in the
1/N_{c}
expansion was introduced in the seminal paper by Witten
witten
. On general grounds, it takes
N_{c}
quarks (in a totally antisymmetric color state) to make up a baryon, so Witten proposed to split the problem into two parts, to first use graphical methods to study
n
-quark forces in the large-
N_{c}
limit, and then to use other methods to analyze the effects of these forces on an
N_{c}
-body state. From the analysis of large-
N_{c}
counting rules for baryon-meson scattering, Witten concluded that the corresponding amplitude at fixed energy must be of order one.

Later on, Dashen and Manohar
dm1
derived a set of consistency conditions that must be satisfied by large-
N_{c}
QCD, and proved that it has a contracted
SU(4)
symmetry (for
N_{f}=2
). The equations obtained from these consistency conditions admit a unique (minimal) solution for the baryon-meson coupling constants, which are identical to those of the Skyrme model or non-relativistic quark model. Dashen, Jenkins, and Manohar applied the approach to show that large-
N_{c}
power counting rules for multimeson–baryon-baryon scattering amplitudes lead to important constraints on baryon static properties
djm94
. On an equal footing, Flores-Mendieta, Hofmann, and Jenkins
rfm00
studied tree-level amplitudes for baryon-meson scattering and obtained generalized large-
N_{c}
consistency conditions valid to all orders in the baryon mass splitting
\Delta\equiv M_{T}-M_{B}
, where
M_{T}
and
M_{B}
represent the baryon decuplet and baryon octet masses, respectively.

In the context of baryon chiral perturbation theory (BChPT), important advancements have been made on baryon-meson scattering over the past three decades. A detailed account of phenomenological models and/or different approaches proposed prior 2016 is presented in Ref.
yao
. Apart from the heavy baryon approach
bernard
;
fettes
, some fully relativistic methods are noteworthy, namely, the infrared regularization of covariant BChPT
becher
and the extended-on-mass-shell scheme for BChPT
geg1
;
geg2
, to name but a few. Further improvements in HBChPT to orders
\mathcal{O}(p^{3})
and
\mathcal{O}(p^{4})
have been performed recently
huangA
;
huangB
.

Despite the important progress achieved in the understanding of baryon-meson scattering processes in both the phenomenological and experimental bent
part
, various challenges still remain unsolved. In view of this, lattice QCD has become an essential non-perturbative tool to tackle some issues with first-principles QCD calculations which can not be dealt with otherwise. A comprehensive description of the state-of-the art computation of scattering amplitude for the baryon-meson system within lattice QCD can be found in Ref.
bulava
. It should be emphasized that only a few lattice studies on the subject are available in the literature.

The present work is part of an ongoing research project to analytically evaluate baryon-meson scattering amplitudes using the
1/N_{c}
expansion. At a first stage, the primary objective will be a tree-level calculation. At a second stage, the effects of
SU(3)
flavor symmetry breaking will be incorporated.

The paper is organized as follows. In Sec.
II
some elementary material about scattering processes is presented, along with an introductory review of large-
N_{c}
QCD to introduce notation and conventions. The
1/N_{c}
expansion of the baryon operator whose matrix elements between baryon states yields the scattering amplitude in constructed. The most complete form of this amplitude is obtained by accounting for the decuplet-octet baryon mass difference explicitly. In Sec.
III
results are particularized to the
N\pi
system; some isospin relations are checked to be respected by these processes. In Sec.
IV
the effects of perturbative
SU(3)
symmetry breaking are evaluated. Some concluding remarks are given in Sec.
VI
. The paper is complemented by three appendices. In Appendix
A
the reduction of the different baryon structures in terms of an operator basis of linearly independent operators is given. In Appendix
B
the full list of the pertinent coefficients that accompany the baryon operators of Appendix
A
is provided. Finally, in Appendix
C
the operator basis used to evaluate SB effects along with their respective matrix elements are detailed.

II
Baryon-meson scattering amplitude at tree level

Here, an analytical computation of the tree-level amplitude of baryon-meson scattering presented in Ref.
rfm00
is explicitly carried out, specialized to the process

B(p)+\pi^{a}(k)\to B^{\prime}(p^{\prime})+\pi^{b}(k^{\prime}).

(1)

The Feynman diagrams representing the process are depicted in Fig.
1
.

Figure 1:
Feynman diagrams for the tree-level scattering process (
1

In Eq. (
1
),
\pi
denotes one of the nine pseudo scalar mesons
\pi
,
K
,
\eta
and
\eta^{\prime}
of momenta
k=(k^{0},k^{1},k^{2},k^{3})
and
k^{\prime}=({k^{\prime}}^{0},{k^{\prime}}^{1},{k^{\prime}}^{2},{k^{\prime}}^{3})
and flavors
a
and
b
for the incoming and outgoing mesons, respectively, and
B
and
B^{\prime}
denote the incoming and outgoing baryons of momenta
p
and
p^{\prime}
, respectively. Soft mesons with energies of order unity are considered in the process. The goal is to explicitly evaluate the corresponding scattering amplitude at tree level, incorporating the effects of the baryon mass splitting
\Delta
defined above. Before tackling the problem, it is convenient to introduce some key concepts on large-
N_{c}
QCD to set notation and conventions. Further details on the formalism can be found in Refs.
djm94
;
djm95
.

In the large-
N_{c}
limit, the baryon sector has a contracted
SU(2N_{f})
spin-flavor symmetry, where
N_{f}
is the number of light quark flavors. For
N_{f}=3
the lowest lying baryon states fall into a representation of the spin-flavor group
SU(6)
. When
N_{c}=3
, this corresponds to the
\mathbf{56}
dimensional representation of
SU(6)
.

The
1/N_{c}
expansion of a QCD operator can be written in terms of
1/N_{c}
-suppressed operators with well-defined spin-flavor transformation properties. A complete set of operators can be constructed using the 0-body operator
\openone
and the 1-body operators

\displaystyle J^{k}

\displaystyle=q^{\dagger}\left[\dfrac{\sigma^{k}}{2}\otimes\openone\right]q,\qquad\qquad(1,1)

(2a)

\displaystyle T^{c}

\displaystyle=q^{\dagger}\left[\openone\otimes\dfrac{\lambda^{c}}{2}\right]q,\qquad\qquad(0,8)

(2b)

\displaystyle G^{kc}

\displaystyle=q^{\dagger}\left[\dfrac{\sigma}{2}\otimes\dfrac{\lambda^{c}}{2}\right]q,\qquad\qquad(1,8)

(2c)

where
J^{k}
,
T^{c}
and
G^{kc}
are the baryon spin, baryon flavor and baryon spin-flavor generators, respectively, which transform under
SU(2)\times SU(3)
as
(j,m)
, where
j
is the spin and
m
is the dimension of the
SU(3)
flavor representation. The
SU(2N_{f})
spin-flavor generators satisfy the commutation relations listed in Table
1

djm95
.

\left[J^{i},T^{a}\right]=0,

\left[J^{i},J^{j}\right]=i\epsilon^{ijk}J^{k},

\left[T^{a},T^{b}\right]=if^{abc}T^{c},

\left[J^{i},G^{ja}\right]=i\epsilon^{ijk}G^{ka},

\left[T^{a},G^{ib}\right]=if^{abc}G^{ic},

\displaystyle[G^{ia},G^{jb}]=\frac{i}{4}\delta^{ij}f^{abc}T^{c}+\frac{i}{2N_{f}}\delta^{ab}\epsilon^{ijk}J^{k}+\frac{i}{2}\epsilon^{ijk}d^{abc}G^{kc}.

Table 1:
SU(2N_{f})
commutation relations.

The Feynman diagrams displayed in Fig.
1
will be analyzed separately as they contribute differently to the scattering process.

II.1
Scattering amplitude from Fig.
1
(a,b)

The baryon operator that yields the tree level amplitude for the scattering process (
1
) represented in Fig.
1
(a,b), in the rest frame of the initial baryon, is given by
rfm00

A_{\mathrm{tree}}^{ab}=-\frac{1}{f^{2}}k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\left[\frac{1}{k^{0}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{{k^{0}}^{n}}[A^{jb},\underbrace{[\mathcal{M},[\mathcal{M},\ldots[\mathcal{M}}_{n\,\,\mathrm{insertions}},A^{ia}\underbrace{]\ldots]]}]\right],

(3)

where
f\approx 93\,\mathrm{MeV}
is the pion decay constant,
A^{ia}
is the baryon axial vector current and
\mathcal{M}
is the baryon mass operator. Explicitly, the
1/N_{c}
expansion of
A^{ia}
, at
N_{c}=3
, is given by
djm95

A^{ia}=a_{1}G^{ia}+\frac{1}{N_{c}}b_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}^{ia}+\frac{1}{N_{c}^{2}}b_{3}\mathcal{D}_{3}^{ia}+\frac{1}{N_{c}^{2}}c_{3}\mathcal{O}_{3}^{ia},

(4)

where
a_{1}
,
b_{2}
,
b_{3}
, and
c_{3}
are unknown coefficients of order one, and the
2
- and
3
-body operators
\mathcal{D}_{2}^{ia}
and
\mathcal{D}_{3}^{ia}
and
\mathcal{O}_{3}^{ia}
read,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{2}^{ia}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J^{i}T^{a},

(5a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{3}^{ia}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{J^{i},\{J^{r},G^{ra}\}\},

(5b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{3}^{ia}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{J^{2},G^{ia}\}-\frac{1}{2}\{J^{i},\{J^{r},G^{ra}\}\}.

(5c)

As for
\mathcal{M}
, it is expressed as
djm95

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}=m_{0}N_{c}\openone+\sum_{n=2,4}^{N_{c}-1}m_{n}\frac{1}{N_{c}^{n-1}}J^{n},

(6)

where
m_{n}
are unknown coefficients. The first term on the right-hand side is the overall spin-independent mass of the baryon multiplet so the remaining spin-dependent terms constitute
\mathcal{M}_{\text{hyperfine}}
. At
N_{c}=3
,
\mathcal{M}_{\text{hyperfine}}
is simply

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\text{hyperfine}}=\frac{m_{2}}{N_{c}}J^{2},

(7)

where
m_{2}
can be set as
m_{2}=\Delta
. Numerically, the average value to be used is
\Delta=0.231
GeV.

The series (
3
) with the first three summands looks like

A_{\mathrm{tree}}^{ab}=-\frac{1}{f^{2}}k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\left[\frac{1}{k^{0}}[A^{jb},A^{ia}]+\frac{1}{{k^{0}}^{2}}[A^{jb},[\mathcal{M},A^{ia}]]+\frac{1}{{k^{0}}^{3}}[A^{jb},[\mathcal{M},[\mathcal{M},A^{ia}]]]+\ldots\right].

(8)

The constraint that
A_{\mathrm{tree}}^{ab}
be at most
\mathcal{O}(1)
in the large-
N_{c}
limit sets the consistency conditions
dm1
;
rfm00

\displaystyle[A^{jb},A^{ia}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}(N_{c}),

(9a)

\displaystyle[A^{jb},[\mathcal{M},A^{ia}]]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}(N_{c}),

(9b)

\displaystyle[A^{jb},[\mathcal{M},[\mathcal{M},A^{ia}]]]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}(N_{c}),

(9c)

where
k^{0}
,
f
, and
\Delta
are orders
\mathcal{O}(1)
,
\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{N_{c}})
, and
\mathcal{O}(N_{c}^{-1})
in that limit, respectively.

II.1.1
Spin-flavor transformation properties of
A_{\mathrm{tree}}^{ab}

The baryon operator
A_{\mathrm{tree}}^{ab}
is a spin-zero object and contains two adjoint (octet) indices. The tensor product of two adjoint representations
\mathbf{8}\otimes\mathbf{8}
can be split into the symmetric product
(\mathbf{8}\otimes\mathbf{8})_{S}
and the antisymmetric product
(\mathbf{8}\otimes\mathbf{8})_{A}

djm95
, which in turn can be decomposed in terms of
SU(3)
multiplets as

\displaystyle(\mathbf{8}\otimes\mathbf{8})_{S}=\mathbf{1}\oplus\mathbf{8}\oplus\mathbf{27},

(10a)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{8}\otimes\mathbf{8})_{A}=\mathbf{8}\oplus\mathbf{10}\oplus\overline{\mathbf{10}}.

(10b)

In order to exploit the transformation properties of
A_{\mathrm{tree}}^{ab}
under the
SU(2)\times SU(3)
spin-flavor symmetry, the spin and flavor projectors introduced in Ref.
banda
become handy. In a few words, this technique exploits the decomposition of the tensor space formed by the product of the adjoint space with itself
n
times,
\prod_{i=1}^{n}adj\otimes
, into subspaces which can be labeled by a specific eigenvalue of the quadratic Casimir operator
C
of the Lie algebra of
SU(N)
. Thus, for each subspace, a projection operator
\mathcal{P}^{(m)}
can be constructed, namely,

\mathcal{P}^{(m)}=\prod_{i=1}^{k}\left[\frac{C-c_{n_{i}}}{c_{m}-c_{n_{i}}}\right],\qquad\qquad c_{m}\neq c_{n_{i}},

(11)

where
k
labels the number of different possible eigenvalues for
C
and
c_{m}
are its eigenvalues given by
Gross
,

c_{m}=\frac{1}{2}\left[nN-\frac{n^{2}}{N}+\sum_{i}r_{i}^{2}-\sum_{i}c^{2}_{i}\right],

(12)

where
n
is the total number of boxes of the Young tableu for a specific representation,
r_{i}
is the number of boxes in the
i
th row and
c_{i}
is the number of boxes in the
i
th column.

In particular, for the product of two
SU(3)
adjoints, the flavor projectors
[\mathcal{P}^{(m)}]^{abcd}
for the irreducible representation of dimension
m
contained in (
10
) are given by

[\mathcal{P}^{(1)}]^{abcd}=\frac{1}{N_{f}^{2}-1}\delta^{ab}\delta^{cd},

(13)

[\mathcal{P}^{(8)}]^{abcd}=\frac{N_{f}}{N_{f}^{2}-4}d^{abe}d^{cde},

(14)

[\mathcal{P}^{(27)}]^{abcd}=\frac{1}{2}(\delta^{ac}\delta^{bd}+\delta^{bc}\delta^{ad})-\frac{1}{N_{f}^{2}-1}\delta^{ab}\delta^{cd}-\frac{N_{f}}{N_{f}^{2}-4}d^{abe}d^{cde},

(15)

[\mathcal{P}^{(8_{A})}]^{abcd}=\frac{1}{N_{f}}f^{abe}f^{cde},

(16)

and

[\mathcal{P}^{(10+\overline{10})}]^{abcd}=\frac{1}{2}(\delta^{ac}\delta^{bd}-\delta^{bc}\delta^{ad})-\frac{1}{N_{f}}f^{abe}f^{cde},

(17)

which fulfill the completeness relation

[\mathcal{P}^{(1)}+\mathcal{P}^{(8)}+\mathcal{P}^{(27)}+\mathcal{P}^{(^{)}}{8_{A}}+\mathcal{P}^{(10+\overline{10})}]^{abcd}=\delta^{ac}\delta^{bd}.

(18)

Therefore,
[\mathcal{P}^{(m)}A_{\mathrm{tree}}]^{ab}
effectively projects out the piece of
A_{\mathrm{tree}}^{ab}
that transforms under the flavor representation of dimension
m
according to decompositions (
10
). However, for computational purposes, it is more convenient to group the operators
[\mathcal{P}^{(m)}A_{\mathrm{tree}}]^{ab}
based on their symmetry transformation properties under the interchange of
a
and
b
. Accordingly,
[\mathcal{P}^{(1)}+\mathcal{P}^{(8)}+\mathcal{P}^{(27)}]^{abcd}
and
[\mathcal{P}^{(8_{A})}+\mathcal{P}^{(10+\overline{10})}]^{abcd}
, acting respectively on the symmetric and antisymmetric [antisymmetric and symmetric] pieces of
A_{\mathrm{tree}}^{cd}
will provide the symmetric [antisymmetric] piece of
A_{\mathrm{tree}}^{ab}
under the interchange of
a
and
b
.

II.1.2
Explicit form of the scattering amplitude at tree-level

A more specialized and detailed calculation beyond the qualitative analyses of baryon-meson scattering presented in previous works
dm1
;
rfm00
can be performed by explicitly evaluating the first summands displayed in Eq. (
8
). The strategy implemented here follows the lines of Refs.
rfm21a
;
rfm21b
; succinctly, all baryon operators allowed at
N_{c}=3
are accounted for in the terms kept in the series (
8
).

As a starting point, it should be recalled that the commutator of an
n
-body operator and an
m
-body operator is an
(n+m-1)
-body operator,
i.e.
,

[O_{m},O_{n}]=O_{m+n-1}.

(19)

A close inspection of Eq. (
8
) reveals that
[A^{jb},A^{ia}]
, for
N_{c}=3
, yields at most the operator structure
[\mathcal{O}_{3}^{jb},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{ia}]
and, according to relation (
19
), retains up to
5
-body operators. Sequential insertions of one and two
J^{2}
operators add up
6
- and
7
-body operators, respectively. Thus, the first three summands displayed in Eq. (
8
) will be explicitly evaluated and they will suffice to draw some conclusions. Clearly, it would be desirable to perform a calculation including 8-body operators and higher, but this is beyond the scope of this work due to the considerable amount of group theory involved.

The task now is to rewrite the baryon operators involved in
[\mathcal{P}^{(m)}A_{\mathrm{tree}}]^{ab}
in terms of a set of linearly independent operators, up to
7
-body operators. After a long, tedious, but otherwise standard calculation, reductions are computed are their results are available. However, due to the length and unilluminating nature of the full expressions, only the symmetric and antisymmetric pieces rather than the individual results for each representation are listed in Appendix
A
. In passing, it is straightforward to verify that the consistency conditions (
9
) are fulfilled by all these reduced structures.

The matrix elements of
A_{\mathrm{tree}}^{ab}
, Eq. (
3
), between
SU(6)
baryon states for mesons of flavors
a
and
b
yield the corresponding scattering amplitude at tree level, namely,

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(B+\pi^{a}\to B^{\prime}+\pi^{b})\equiv\langle B^{\prime}\pi^{b}|A_{\mathrm{tree}}^{ab}|B\pi^{a}\rangle.

(20)

The flavors associated to mesons are conventionally given by
\Big\{\frac{1-i2}{\sqrt{2}},3,\frac{1+i2}{\sqrt{2}},\frac{4-i5}{\sqrt{2}},\frac{6-i7}{\sqrt{2}},\frac{4+i5}{\sqrt{2}},\frac{6+i7}{\sqrt{2}},8\Big\}
for
\{\pi^{+},\pi^{0},\pi^{-},K^{+},K^{0},K^{-},\overline{K}^{0},\eta\}
, respectively.
1
1

1

For simplicity only the octet of pseudo scalar mesons is considered. Extending the analysis to include the
\eta^{\prime}
is straightforward by using the baryon axial vector current
A^{i}\equiv A^{i9}
, which is written in terms of the 1-body operators
G^{i9}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}J^{i}
and
T^{9}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}N_{c}\openone

jen96
.
For instance, an expressions such as
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{0})
corresponds to
\langle n\pi^{0}|A_{\mathrm{tree}}^{13}+iA_{\mathrm{tree}}^{23}|p\pi^{-}\rangle/\sqrt{2}
.

Thus, with the operator reductions listed in Appendix
A
, the scattering amplitude for process (
1
), arising from Fig.
1
(a,b), can be organized as

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(B+\pi^{a}\to B^{\prime}+\pi^{b})=-\frac{1}{f^{2}k^{0}}\sum_{m=1}^{139}(c^{(\mathrm{s})}_{m}+c^{(\mathrm{a})}_{m})k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\langle B^{\prime}\pi^{b}|S_{m}^{(ij)(ab)}|B\pi^{a}\rangle,

(21)

where
S_{m}^{(ij)(ab)}
(
m=1,\ldots,139
) constitute a basis of linearly independent spin-2 baryon operators with two adjoint indices and
c^{(\mathrm{s})}_{m}
and
c^{(\mathrm{a})}_{m}
are well-defined coefficients which come along with the symmetric and antisymmetric pieces of
A^{ab}
. Notice that in Eq. (
21
) the sum over spin indices is implicit.

The operators
S_{m}^{(ij)(ab)}
that constitute the basis, comprising up to
7
-body operators, read

\displaystyle S_{1}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=i\delta^{ij}f^{abe}T^{e},

\displaystyle S_{2}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=i\epsilon^{ijr}\delta^{ab}J^{r},

\displaystyle S_{3}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=i\epsilon^{ijr}d^{abe}G^{re},

\displaystyle S_{4}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=\delta^{ab}\{J^{i},J^{j}\},

\displaystyle S_{5}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=\delta^{ij}\delta^{ab}J^{2},

\displaystyle S_{6}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=\{G^{ia},G^{jb}\},

\displaystyle S_{7}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=\{G^{ib},G^{ja}\},

\displaystyle S_{8}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=\delta^{ij}\{G^{ra},G^{rb}\},

\displaystyle S_{9}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=i\epsilon^{ijr}\{G^{ra},T^{b}\},

\displaystyle S_{10}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=i\epsilon^{ijr}\{G^{rb},T^{a}\},

\displaystyle S_{11}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=d^{abe}\{J^{j},G^{ie}\},

\displaystyle S_{12}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=if^{abe}\{J^{i},G^{je}\},

\displaystyle S_{13}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=if^{abe}\{J^{j},G^{ie}\},

\displaystyle S_{14}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=\delta^{ij}d^{abe}\{J^{r},G^{re}\},

\displaystyle S_{15}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{ijr}f^{abe}\mathcal{D}_{2}^{re},

\displaystyle S_{16}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=i\epsilon^{ijr}d^{abe}\mathcal{D}_{3}^{re},

\displaystyle S_{17}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=i\epsilon^{ijr}d^{abe}\mathcal{O}_{3}^{re},

\displaystyle S_{18}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=i\epsilon^{ijr}\{J^{r},\{T^{a},T^{b}\}\},

\displaystyle S_{19}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=i\epsilon^{ijm}\{J^{m},\{G^{ra},G^{rb}\}\},

\displaystyle S_{20}^{(ij)(ab)}

\displaystyle=if^{abe}\{T^{e},\{J^{i},J^{j}\}\},

\displaystyle S_{21}^{(ij)(ab)}

II.2
Scattering amplitude from Fig.
1
(c)

The 2-meson-baryon-baryon contact interaction represented in Fig.
1
(c) contributes to the baryon-meson scattering amplitude with a term
rfm00

A_{\mathrm{vertex}}^{ab}=-\frac{1}{2f^{2}}(2k^{0}+M-M^{\prime})f^{abc}T^{c},

(23)

where
M
and
M^{\prime}
denote the masses of the initial and final baryons, respectively. Since
A_{\mathrm{vertex}}^{ab}
is already antisymmetric under the interchange of
a
and
b
, the only term that remains once the projection operators are applied is
[\mathcal{P}^{(8_{A})}A_{\mathrm{vertex}}]^{ab}
, so this term only contributes to the octet piece.
A_{\mathrm{vertex}}^{ab}
is also order
\mathcal{O}(1)
, so together with
A_{\mathrm{tree}}^{ab}
Eq. (
3
), they both yield the leading order
\mathcal{O}(1)
scattering amplitude for baryons with spin
J\sim\mathcal{O}(1)
in the limit of exact
SU(3)
flavor symmetry.

III
Application:
N\pi\to N\pi
scattering amplitude

The formalism presented so far can be implemented to study scattering processes of the form
B+\pi^{a}\to B^{\prime}+\pi^{b}
provided that
reactions in which these particles are produced have equal total strangeness on each side, according to the Gell-Mann–Nishijima scheme. Since
B
and
B^{\prime}
can be either octet or decuplet baryons, from the theoretical point of view the possibilities are numerous; examples are
\Lambda+K^{+}\to p+\pi^{0}
,
{\Xi^{*}}^{-}+K^{+}\to{\Sigma^{*}}^{0}+\pi^{0}
,
{\Xi^{*}}^{-}+K^{0}\to\Sigma^{-}+\pi^{0}
, and so on. For definiteness, the
N\pi\to N\pi
scattering processes will be analyzed to exemplify the approach.

A pion,
T=1
, and a nucleon,
T=\frac{1}{2}
, can be combined in a
T=\frac{3}{2}
or a
T=\frac{1}{2}
state following the usual addition rules of angular momenta
add
. The allowed states for the
N\pi
system are listed in Table
2
.

Table 2:
Allowed states for
N\pi
system.

T=\frac{3}{2}

T=\frac{1}{2}

T_{3}=+\frac{3}{2}

|p\pi^{+}\rangle

T_{3}=+\frac{1}{2}

\sqrt{\frac{1}{3}}|n\pi^{+}\rangle+\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}|p\pi^{0}\rangle

\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}|n\pi^{+}\rangle-\sqrt{\frac{1}{3}}|p\pi^{0}\rangle

T_{3}=-\frac{1}{2}

\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}|n\pi^{0}\rangle+\sqrt{\frac{1}{3}}|p\pi^{-}\rangle

\sqrt{\frac{1}{3}}|n\pi^{0}\rangle-\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}|p\pi^{-}\rangle

T_{3}=-\frac{3}{2}

|n\pi^{-}\rangle

The elastic scattering amplitude for the process (
1
) can be decomposed, by the usual Clebsh-Gordan technique, into two non-interfering amplitudes,
\mathcal{A}^{(T)}
, with
T=\frac{3}{2}
and
T=\frac{1}{2}
. Thus, starting from the
s
-channel isospin eigenstates

\displaystyle|p\pi^{+}\rangle=\left|\frac{3}{2},+\frac{3}{2}\right\rangle,

(24)

\displaystyle|n\pi^{+}\rangle=\sqrt{\frac{1}{3}}\left|\frac{3}{2},+\frac{1}{2}\right\rangle+\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\left|\frac{1}{2},+\frac{1}{2}\right\rangle,

(25)

\displaystyle|p\pi^{0}\rangle=\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\left|\frac{3}{2},+\frac{1}{2}\right\rangle-\sqrt{\frac{1}{3}}\left|\frac{1}{2},+\frac{1}{2}\right\rangle,

(26)

\displaystyle|n\pi^{0}\rangle=\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\left|\frac{3}{2},-\frac{1}{2}\right\rangle+\sqrt{\frac{1}{3}}\left|\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2}\right\rangle,

(27)

\displaystyle|p\pi^{-}\rangle=\sqrt{\frac{1}{3}}\left|\frac{3}{2},-\frac{1}{2}\right\rangle-\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\left|\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2}\right\rangle,

(28)

\displaystyle|n\pi^{-}\rangle=\left|\frac{3}{2},-\frac{3}{2}\right\rangle,

(29)

it is straightforward to obtain
ditsche

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{+}\to p+\pi^{+})=\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{-})=\mathcal{A}^{(3/2)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{-}\to p+\pi^{-})=\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{+}\to n+\pi^{+})=\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{A}^{(3/2)}+\frac{2}{3}\mathcal{A}^{(1/2)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{0}\to p+\pi^{0})=\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{0}\to n+\pi^{0})=\frac{2}{3}\mathcal{A}^{(3/2)}+\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{A}^{(1/2)},

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{0})=\sqrt{2}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{+}\to p+\pi^{0})=\frac{2}{3}\mathcal{A}^{(3/2)}-\frac{2}{3}\mathcal{A}^{(1/2)}.

(30)

Furthermore, an alternative set of invariant amplitudes,
\mathcal{A}^{(+)}
and
\mathcal{A}^{(-)}
, can be introduced for the
N\pi
system, which are defined as
moo

\mathcal{A}^{(+)}=\frac{2}{3}\mathcal{A}^{(3/2)}+\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{A}^{(1/2)},\qquad\mathcal{A}^{(-)}=-\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{A}^{(3/2)}+\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{A}^{(1/2)},

(31)

so

\mathcal{A}^{(3/2)}=\mathcal{A}^{(+)}-\mathcal{A}^{(-)},\qquad\mathcal{A}^{(1/2)}=\mathcal{A}^{(+)}+2\mathcal{A}^{(-)}.

(32)

The non-trivial matrix elements
k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\langle B^{\prime}\pi^{b}|S_{r}^{(ij)(ab)}|B\pi^{a}\rangle
are displayed in Tables
3
and
4
for proton-pion and neutron-pion processes (
N\pi\to N\pi
processes for short), respectively.
2
2

2

Here, non-trivial matrix elements are those which are either zero or obtained as anticommutators with
J^{2}
. For instance, for the
N\pi
system,
k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\langle B^{\prime}\pi^{b}|S_{17}^{(ij)(ab)}|B\pi^{a}\rangle
vanishes whereas
k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\langle B^{\prime}\pi^{b}|S_{37}^{(ij)(ab)}|B\pi^{a}\rangle=\frac{3}{2}k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\langle B^{\prime}\pi^{b}|S_{15}^{(ij)(ab)}|B\pi^{a}\rangle
, so they are not listed.
It can be easily verified that the symmetric and antisymmetric pieces of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(B+\pi^{a}\to B^{\prime}+\pi^{b})
are respectively proportional to
\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
and the third component of
i(\mathbf{k}\times\mathbf{k}^{\prime})
, which will be denoted hereafter by
i(\mathbf{k}\times\mathbf{k}^{\prime})^{3}
. The latter can also be rewritten as
i\epsilon^{ij3}k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}=i(k^{1}{k^{\prime}}^{2}-k^{2}{k^{\prime}}^{1})
.

) the tree-level scattering amplitude for the
N\pi
system can be cast into

\displaystyle f^{2}k^{0}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{+}\to p+\pi^{+})

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 5.69046pt}=\left[-\frac{25}{72}a_{1}^{2}-\frac{5}{36}a_{1}b_{2}-\frac{25}{108}a_{1}b_{3}-\frac{1}{72}b_{2}^{2}-\frac{5}{108}b_{2}b_{3}-\frac{25}{648}b_{3}^{2}+\frac{2}{9}\left[1-\frac{2\Delta}{k^{0}}+\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{k^{0}}^{2}}\right]\left[a_{1}^{2}+a_{1}c_{3}+\frac{1}{4}c_{3}^{2}\right]\right]\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{k}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\left[\frac{25}{72}a_{1}^{2}+\frac{5}{36}a_{1}b_{2}+\frac{25}{108}a_{1}b_{3}+\frac{1}{72}b_{2}^{2}+\frac{5}{108}b_{2}b_{3}+\frac{25}{648}b_{3}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 31.2982pt}\left.-\frac{2}{9}\left[1-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Delta}{k^{0}}+\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{k^{0}}^{2}}\right]\left[a_{1}^{2}+a_{1}c_{3}+\frac{1}{4}c_{3}^{2}\right]\right]i(\mathbf{k}\times\mathbf{k}^{\prime})^{3}+\mathcal{O}\left[\frac{\Delta^{3}}{{k^{0}}^{3}}\right]

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 5.69046pt}=f^{2}k^{0}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{-}),

(33)

\displaystyle f^{2}k^{0}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{-}\to p+\pi^{-})

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 5.69046pt}=\left[\frac{25}{72}a_{1}^{2}+\frac{5}{36}a_{1}b_{2}+\frac{25}{108}a_{1}b_{3}+\frac{1}{72}b_{2}^{2}+\frac{5}{108}b_{2}b_{3}+\frac{25}{648}b_{3}^{2}-\frac{2}{9}\left[1+\frac{2\Delta}{k^{0}}+\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{k^{0}}^{2}}\right]\left[a_{1}^{2}+a_{1}c_{3}+\frac{1}{4}c_{3}^{2}\right]\right]\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{k}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\left[\frac{25}{72}a_{1}^{2}+\frac{5}{36}a_{1}b_{2}+\frac{25}{108}a_{1}b_{3}+\frac{1}{72}b_{2}^{2}+\frac{5}{108}b_{2}b_{3}+\frac{25}{648}b_{3}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 31.2982pt}\left.-\frac{2}{9}\left[1+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Delta}{k^{0}}+\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{k^{0}}^{2}}\right]\left[a_{1}^{2}+a_{1}c_{3}+\frac{1}{4}c_{3}^{2}\right]\right]i(\mathbf{k}\times\mathbf{k}^{\prime})^{3}+\mathcal{O}\left[\frac{\Delta^{3}}{{k^{0}}^{3}}\right]

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 5.69046pt}=-\frac{4}{9}\frac{\Delta}{k^{0}}\left[a_{1}^{2}+a_{1}c_{3}+\frac{1}{4}c_{3}^{2}\right]\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\left[\frac{25}{72}a_{1}^{2}+\frac{5}{36}a_{1}b_{2}+\frac{25}{108}a_{1}b_{3}+\frac{1}{72}b_{2}^{2}+\frac{5}{108}b_{2}b_{3}+\frac{25}{648}b_{3}^{2}\right.

(35)

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}f^{2}k^{0}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{0})

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 5.69046pt}=\left[-\frac{25}{36}a_{1}^{2}-\frac{5}{18}a_{1}b_{2}-\frac{25}{54}a_{1}b_{3}-\frac{1}{36}b_{2}^{2}-\frac{5}{54}b_{2}b_{3}-\frac{25}{324}b_{3}^{2}+\frac{4}{9}\left[1+\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{k^{0}}^{2}}\right]\left[a_{1}^{2}+a_{1}c_{3}+\frac{1}{4}c_{3}^{2}\right]\right]\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{k}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 5.69046pt}=\sqrt{2}f^{2}k^{0}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{+}\to p+\pi^{0}).

(36)

The above results can be rewritten in terms of the

introduced in HBChPT
jm255

jen96

\displaystyle D=\frac{1}{2}a_{1}+\frac{1}{6}b_{3},

(37a)

\displaystyle F=\frac{1}{3}a_{1}+\frac{1}{6}b_{2}+\frac{1}{9}b_{3},

(37b)

(37c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}=-\frac{3}{2}a_{1}-\frac{3}{2}b_{2}-\frac{5}{2}b_{3}.

(37d)

A further simplification can be achieved if the power series expansion in

of the function

t_{1}\frac{k^{0}}{k^{0}-\Delta}+t_{2}\frac{k^{0}}{k^{0}+\Delta}=t_{1}+t_{2}+(t_{1}-t_{2})\frac{\Delta}{k^{0}}+(t_{1}+t_{2})\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{k^{0}}^{2}}+(t_{1}-t_{2})\frac{\Delta^{3}}{{k^{0}}^{3}}+\mathcal{O}\left[\frac{\Delta^{4}}{{k^{0}}^{4}}\right],

\displaystyle f^{2}k^{0}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{+}\to p+\pi^{+})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[-\frac{1}{2}(D+F)^{2}+\frac{1}{9}\left[-\frac{k^{0}}{k^{0}-\Delta}+3\frac{k^{0}}{k^{0}+\Delta}\right]\mathcal{C}^{2}\right]\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{k}^{\prime}

(39)

\displaystyle\mbox{}+\left[\frac{1}{2}(D+F)^{2}-\frac{1}{18}\left[\frac{k^{0}}{k^{0}-\Delta}+3\frac{k^{0}}{k^{0}+\Delta}\right]\mathcal{C}^{2}\right]i(\mathbf{k}\times\mathbf{k}^{\prime})^{3}

\displaystyle\mbox{}f^{2}k^{0}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{-}),

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}f^{2}k^{0}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{+}\to p+\pi^{0}),

which are valid to order

.

A glance at the above expressions shows that the scattering amplitudes for
N\pi\to N\pi
processes are written in terms of the

,

, and

;

, a totally expected and consistent result because

and

is the axial coupling for neutron beta decay, whereas

comes along

vertices. Also, in the limit
\Delta\to 0
the coefficients of the
\mathcal{C}^{2}
terms do not vanish.

As for the

\displaystyle f^{2}k^{0}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}^{(1/2)}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\left[\frac{25}{72}a_{1}^{2}+\frac{5}{36}a_{1}b_{2}+\frac{25}{108}a_{1}b_{3}+\frac{1}{72}b_{2}^{2}+\frac{5}{108}b_{2}b_{3}+\frac{25}{648}b_{3}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}\left.-\frac{2}{9}\left[1+\frac{\Delta}{k^{0}}+\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{k^{0}}^{2}}\right]\left[a_{1}^{2}+a_{1}c_{3}+\frac{1}{4}c_{3}^{2}\right]\right]i(\mathbf{k}\times\mathbf{k}^{\prime})^{3}+\mathcal{O}\left[\frac{\Delta^{3}}{{k^{0}}^{3}}\right],

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\left[\frac{25}{72}a_{1}^{2}+\frac{5}{36}a_{1}b_{2}+\frac{25}{108}a_{1}b_{3}+\frac{1}{72}b_{2}^{2}+\frac{5}{108}b_{2}b_{3}+\frac{25}{648}b_{3}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}\left.-\frac{2}{9}\left[1-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Delta}{k^{0}}+\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{k^{0}}^{2}}\right]\left[a_{1}^{2}+a_{1}c_{3}+\frac{1}{4}c_{3}^{2}\right]\right]i(\mathbf{k}\times\mathbf{k}^{\prime})^{3}+\mathcal{O}\left[\frac{\Delta^{3}}{{k^{0}}^{3}}\right],

III.1.1
Isospin relations

The
N\pi\to N\pi
tree-level scattering amplitudes satisfy the following isospin relations,

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{-}\to p+\pi^{-})-\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{0}\to p+\pi^{0})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{0})=0,

(47)

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{+}\to p+\pi^{+})-\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{-}\to p+\pi^{-})-\sqrt{2}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{0})=0,

(48)

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{+}\to p+\pi^{+})+\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{-}\to p+\pi^{-})-2\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(p+\pi^{0}\to p+\pi^{0})=0,

(49)

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{-})-\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{0}\to n+\pi^{0})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{+}\to p+\pi^{0})=0,

(50)

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{+}\to n+\pi^{+})-\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{-})+\sqrt{2}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{+}\to p+\pi^{0})=0,

(51)

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{+}\to n+\pi^{+})+\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{-})-2\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}(n+\pi^{0}\to n+\pi^{0})=0.

(52)

(c)

IV
First-order
SU(3)
flavor symmetry breaking in the scattering amplitude

The scattering amplitude obtained so far is given in the exact
SU(3)
symmetry limit. This limit, however, represents a rough approximation to the actual expressions, which should contain the effects of perturbative flavor
SU(3)
symmetry breaking (SB). SB in QCD arises from the light quark masses and transforms as a flavor adjoint. In this section, effects due to SB to the scattering amplitude are discussed for the diagrams displayed in Fig.
1
(a,b) and
1
(c) separately as they involve different operator structures.

IV.1
SB effects to the scattering amplitude from Fig.
1
(a,b).

First-order SB effects in the scattering amplitude are computed from the tensor product of the scattering amplitude itself, which transforms under
SU(2)\times SU(3)
as
(2,\mathbf{8}\otimes\mathbf{8})
, and the perturbation, which transforms as
(0,\mathbf{8})
. The tensor product of three adjoint representations
\mathbf{8}\otimes\mathbf{8}\otimes\mathbf{8}
decomposes as

\mathbf{8}\otimes\mathbf{8}\otimes\mathbf{8}=2(\mathbf{1})\oplus 8(\mathbf{8})\oplus 4(\mathbf{10}\oplus\overline{\mathbf{10}})\oplus 6(\mathbf{27})\oplus 2(\mathbf{35}\oplus\overline{\mathbf{35}})\oplus\mathbf{64}.

(63)

Thus, effects of SB can be evaluated by constructing the
1/N_{c}
expansions of the pieces of the scattering amplitude transforming as
(2,\mathbf{1})
,
(2,\mathbf{8})
,
(2,\mathbf{10}\oplus\overline{\mathbf{10}})
,
(2,\mathbf{27})
,
(2,\mathbf{35}\oplus\overline{\mathbf{35}})
and
(2,\mathbf{64})
under
SU(2)\times SU(3)
. These
1/N_{c}
expansions need be expressed in terms of a complete basis of linearly independent operators
\{R^{(ij)(a_{1}a_{2}a_{3})}\}
, where a generic operator
R^{(ij)(a_{1}a_{2}a_{3})}
thus represents a spin-2 object with three adjoint indices. For
N_{c}=3
, up to 3-body operators should be retained in the series. Accordingly, first-order SB can be accounted for by setting one of the flavor indices to 8,
v.gr.
,
a_{3}=8
. For completeness, the set of up to 3-body operators used as a basis is listed in Appendix
C
. The set contains 170 linearly independent operators.

The task of constructing the operators that yield SB effects facilitates considerably again with the implementation of the projection operator technique presented in Ref.
banda
. In this case, for the decomposition (
63
), the projection operators can be constructed as

\left[\mathcal{P}^{(m)}\right]^{c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}b_{1}b_{2}b_{3}}=\left[\left(\frac{C-c^{n_{1}}\openone}{c^{m}-c^{n_{1}}}\right)\left(\frac{C-c^{n_{2}}\openone}{c^{m}-c^{n_{2}}}\right)\left(\frac{C-c^{n_{3}}\openone}{c^{m}-c^{n_{3}}}\right)\left(\frac{C-c^{n_{4}}\openone}{c^{m}-c^{n_{4}}}\right)\left(\frac{C-c^{n_{5}}\openone}{c^{m}-c^{n_{5}}}\right)\right]^{c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}b_{1}b_{2}b_{3}},

(64)

where
m
labels the flavor representation of each projector and
n_{i}
label flavor representations other than
m
. The quadratic Casimir operator, in turn, reads

[C]^{c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}b_{1}b_{2}b_{3}}=6\delta^{c_{1}b_{1}}\delta^{c_{2}b_{2}}\delta^{c_{3}b_{3}}-2\delta^{c_{1}b_{1}}f^{ac_{2}b_{2}}f^{ac_{3}b_{3}}-2\delta^{c_{2}b_{2}}f^{ac_{1}b_{1}}f^{ac_{3}b_{3}}-2\delta^{c_{3}b_{3}}f^{ac_{1}b_{1}}f^{ac_{2}b_{2}},

(65)

and

c^{1}=0,\hskip 14.22636ptc^{8}=3,\hskip 14.22636ptc^{10+\overline{10}}=6,\hskip 14.22636ptc^{27}=8,\hskip 14.22636ptc^{35+\overline{35}}=12,\hskip 14.22636ptc^{64}=15,

(66)

are its corresponding eigenvalues.

Therefore, the product
\left[\mathcal{P}^{(m)}R^{(ij)}\right]^{c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}
will effectively provide the component of the operator
R^{(ij)(c_{1}c_{2}c_{3})}
transforming in the irreducible representation of dimension
m
, according to decomposition (
63
).

The explicit analytic construction of
\left[\mathcal{P}^{(m)}\right]^{c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}b_{1}b_{2}b_{3}}
, however, faces several algebraic challenges. The most relevant one is how to deal with the products of up to ten
f
symbols contained in the
C^{5}
operator, which, in general, can not be reduced in terms containing fewer
f
of
d
symbols. Thus, the algebraic forms of
\left[\mathcal{P}^{(m)}\right]^{c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}b_{1}b_{2}b_{3}}
contain several hundreds of terms, which, in practice, become unmanageable. A more pragmatic approach should be adopted to solve the problem; it turns out that a matrix numerical method is the most suitable one for this purpose.

To start with, notice that each projection operator (or quadratic Casimir operator) is an object with six adjoint indices, each one with eight possible values, so all these objects have
8^{6}
elements. However, Casimir operators have all or half of their indices contracted and the projectors are applied on
3
-body operators with three adjoint indices, so half of the projector indices will be always contracted. Therefore, it is possible to collect the first three indices (
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
,
c_{3}
) and the last three indices (
b_{1}
,
b_{2}
,
b_{3}
) of both the Casimir and projectors in only two indices, one for each set. These new indices have
8^{3}=512
values. In this way, a matrix representation for the projectors is achieved, which is constituted by
512\times 512
matrices. Similarly, the
3
-body operators with three adjoint indices can be represented as vectors with
512
entries. Therefore, instead of performing the index contractions
\left[\mathcal{P}^{(m)}R^{(ij)}\right]^{c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}
, the problem reduces to ordinary matrix multiplications. The whole procedure does not yield to any loss of information and effectively simplifies the analysis.

Let
{\sf P}^{(m)}
be the matrix corresponding to the projection operator
\left[\mathcal{P}^{(m)}\right]^{c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}b_{1}b_{2}b_{3}}
. With the method implemented, a series of consistency checks have been performed, namely,

{\sf P}^{(m)}{\sf P}^{(m)}={\sf P}^{(m)},\qquad\qquad{\sf P}^{(m)}{\sf P}^{(n)}=0,\quad n\neq m,

(67)

along with

{\sf P}^{(1)}+{\sf P}^{(8)}+{\sf P}^{(10+\overline{10})}+{\sf P}^{(27)}+{\sf P}^{(35+\overline{35})}+{\sf P}^{(64)}={\sf I}_{512},

(68)

where
{\sf I}_{512}
stands for the identity matrix of order
512
. The above relations are the usual properties that projection operatators must satisfy. No further details on the method are needed here.

The way these flavor projection operators work can be better seen through a few examples. The operator
\{T^{a},\{T^{b},T^{c}\}\}
, for instance, contributes to the scattering amplitude of the process
n+\pi^{+}\to n+\pi^{+}
through the components with flavor indices
a=(1-i2)/\sqrt{2}
,
b=(1-i2)/\sqrt{2}
, and
c=8
. Using the matrix method, the (118) component of the flavor
{\mathbf{8}}
piece becomes,

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{P}^{(8)}}]^{118cde}\{T^{c},\{T^{d},T^{e}\}\}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 5.69046pt}=\frac{1}{15}T^{1}T^{1}T^{8}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{1}T^{4}T^{6}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{1}T^{5}T^{7}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{1}T^{6}T^{4}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{1}T^{7}T^{5}+\frac{4}{15}T^{1}T^{8}T^{1}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{1}{15}T^{2}T^{2}T^{8}-\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{2}T^{4}T^{7}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{2}T^{5}T^{6}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{2}T^{6}T^{5}-\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{2}T^{7}T^{4}+\frac{4}{15}T^{2}T^{8}T^{2}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{1}{15}T^{3}T^{3}T^{8}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{3}T^{4}T^{4}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{3}T^{5}T^{5}-\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{3}T^{6}T^{6}-\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{3}T^{7}T^{7}+\frac{4}{15}T^{3}T^{8}T^{3}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}-\frac{1}{15\sqrt{3}}T^{4}T^{1}T^{6}+\frac{1}{15\sqrt{3}}T^{4}T^{2}T^{7}-\frac{1}{15\sqrt{3}}T^{4}T^{3}T^{4}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{4}T^{4}T^{3}+\frac{1}{10}T^{4}T^{4}T^{8}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{4}T^{6}T^{1}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}-\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{4}T^{7}T^{2}+\frac{1}{5}T^{4}T^{8}T^{4}-\frac{1}{15\sqrt{3}}T^{5}T^{1}T^{7}-\frac{1}{15\sqrt{3}}T^{5}T^{2}T^{6}-\frac{1}{15\sqrt{3}}T^{5}T^{3}T^{5}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{5}T^{5}T^{3}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{1}{10}T^{5}T^{5}T^{8}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{5}T^{6}T^{2}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{5}T^{7}T^{1}+\frac{1}{5}T^{5}T^{8}T^{5}-\frac{1}{15\sqrt{3}}T^{6}T^{1}T^{4}-\frac{1}{15\sqrt{3}}T^{6}T^{2}T^{5}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{1}{15\sqrt{3}}T^{6}T^{3}T^{6}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{6}T^{4}T^{1}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{6}T^{5}T^{2}-\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{6}T^{6}T^{3}+\frac{1}{10}T^{6}T^{6}T^{8}+\frac{1}{5}T^{6}T^{8}T^{6}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}-\frac{1}{15\sqrt{3}}T^{7}T^{1}T^{5}+\frac{1}{15\sqrt{3}}T^{7}T^{2}T^{4}+\frac{1}{15\sqrt{3}}T^{7}T^{3}T^{7}-\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{7}T^{4}T^{2}+\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{7}T^{5}T^{1}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}-\frac{1}{30\sqrt{3}}T^{7}T^{7}T^{3}+\frac{1}{10}T^{7}T^{7}T^{8}+\frac{1}{5}T^{7}T^{8}T^{7}+\frac{1}{15}T^{8}T^{1}T^{1}+\frac{1}{15}T^{8}T^{2}T^{2}+\frac{1}{15}T^{8}T^{3}T^{3}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{1}{10}T^{8}T^{4}T^{4}+\frac{1}{10}T^{8}T^{5}T^{5}+\frac{1}{10}T^{8}T^{6}T^{6}+\frac{1}{10}T^{8}T^{7}T^{7}+\frac{2}{5}T^{8}T^{8}T^{8}.

(69)

Similar expressions to Eq. (
69
) can be found for the (228), (128) and (218) components required in the example so it can be shown that

[\mathcal{P}^{(1)}+\mathcal{P}^{(8)}+\mathcal{P}^{(10+\overline{10})}+\mathcal{P}^{(27)}+\mathcal{P}^{(35+\overline{35})}+\mathcal{P}^{(64)}]^{118cde}\{T^{c},\{T^{d},T^{e}\}\}=\{T^{1},\{T^{1},T^{8}\}\},

(70)

which is the expected result. Computing the matrix elements of the operator (
69
) is straightforward; therefore,

\langle n\pi^{+}|[{\mathcal{P}^{(8)}}]^{118cde}\{T^{c},\{T^{d},T^{e}\}\}|n\pi^{+}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{3},

(71)

and

\langle n\pi^{+}|[{\mathcal{P}^{(r)}}]^{118cde}\{T^{c},\{T^{d},T^{e}\}\}|n\pi^{+}\rangle=0,

(72)

for
r\neq 8
.

The procedure can be repeated for each flavor combination so the different contributions of the operator
\{T^{a},\{T^{b},T^{c}\}\}
to the scattering amplitude of the process
n+\pi^{+}\to n+\pi^{+}
can be available. For the canonical example, the final expression can be summarized as

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\delta^{ij}\langle n\pi^{+}|[{\mathcal{P}^{(8)}}]^{(1-i2)(1-i2)8cde}\{T^{c},\{T^{d},T^{e}\}\}|n\pi^{+}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{3}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{k}^{\prime},

(73)

and

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\delta^{ij}\left[\langle n\pi^{+}|[{P^{(m)}}]^{(1-i2)(1-i2)8cde}\right]\{T^{c},\{T^{d},T^{e}\}\}|n\pi^{+}\rangle=0,

(74)

for
r\neq 8
.

After a long but otherwise standard calculation, first-order SB to the scattering amplitude
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{tree}}
Eq. (
21
), denoted hereafter by
\delta\mathcal{A}
, can be organized as

\displaystyle f^{2}k^{0}\delta\mathcal{A}(B+\pi^{a}\to B^{\prime}+\pi^{b})=

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 5.69046pt}\sum_{m}\Big[N_{c}g_{1}^{(m)}k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\langle B^{\prime}\pi^{b}|[\mathcal{P}^{(m)}R_{1}^{(ij)}]^{(ab8)}|B\pi^{a}\rangle+N_{c}g_{2}^{(m)}k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\langle B^{\prime}\pi^{b}|[\mathcal{P}^{(m)}R_{2}^{(ij)}]^{(ab8)}|B\pi^{a}\rangle

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\sum_{r=3}^{16}g_{r}^{(m)}k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\langle B^{\prime}\pi^{b}|[\mathcal{P}^{(m)}R_{r}^{(ij)}]^{(ab8)}|B\pi^{a}\rangle+\frac{1}{N_{c}}\sum_{r=17}^{71}g_{r}^{(m)}k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\langle B^{\prime}\pi^{b}|[\mathcal{P}^{(m)}R_{r}^{(ij)}]^{(ab8)}|B\pi^{a}\rangle

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{1}{N_{c}^{2}}\sum_{r=72}^{170}g_{r}^{(m)}k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\langle B^{\prime}\pi^{b}|[\mathcal{P}^{(m)}R_{r}^{(ij)}]^{(ab8)}|B\pi^{a}\rangle\Big],

(75)

where
g_{r}^{(m)}
,
r=1,\ldots,170
, are undetermined coefficients, which are expected to be of order one, the sum over
m
covers all six irreducible representations indicated in relation (
63
) and the sums over
i
and
j
are implicit.

Despite the lengthy structure of Eq. (
75
), with over a thousand of unknown coefficients, further simplifications can be achieved. For the
N+\pi\to N+\pi
process exemplified so far, it turns out that some rearrangements of coefficients are possible so that simpler expressions are obtained.

For instance, the flavor
{\mathbf{1}}
piece of
\delta\mathcal{A}(n+\pi^{+}\to n+\pi^{+})
, using the corresponding matrix elements of the operators listed in Appendix
C
, becomes

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{3}f^{2}k^{0}\delta\mathcal{A}^{(1)}(n+\pi^{+}\to n+\pi^{+})

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 5.69046pt}=\Big[6g_{2}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{3}{g_{18}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{2}g_{20}^{(1)}}+\frac{1}{2}g_{52}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{2}g_{53}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{2}g_{54}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{18}g_{95}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{18}g_{96}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{18}g_{97}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{18}g_{98}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{18}g_{99}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{1}{18}g_{100}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{3}g_{110}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{3}g_{111}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{3}g_{112}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{9}g_{116}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{9}g_{117}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{9}g_{118}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{9}g_{119}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{9}g_{120}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{9}g_{121}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{134}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{1}{6}g_{135}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{136}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{137}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{138}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{139}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{140}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{141}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{142}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{143}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{144}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{145}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}-\frac{1}{18}g_{146}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{18}g_{147}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{18}g_{148}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{18}g_{149}^{(1)}\Big]\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\Big[g_{4}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{63}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{64}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{65}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{66}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{67}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{1}{6}g_{68}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{73}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{80}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{81}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{6}g_{82}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{12}g_{122}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{12}g_{123}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{12}g_{124}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{8}g_{125}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{8}g_{126}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{8}g_{127}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}-\frac{1}{24}g_{128}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{24}g_{129}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{24}g_{130}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{16}g_{150}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{16}g_{151}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{16}g_{152}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{16}g_{153}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{16}g_{154}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{16}g_{155}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{16}g_{156}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}-\frac{1}{16}g_{157}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{16}g_{158}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{16}g_{159}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{16}g_{160}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{16}g_{161}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{16}g_{162}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{16}g_{163}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{16}g_{164}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{16}g_{165}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{16}g_{166}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{1}{16}g_{167}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{16}g_{168}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{16}g_{169}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{16}g_{170}^{(1)}\Big]i(\mathbf{k}\times\mathbf{k}^{\prime})^{3}.

(76)

The applicability of expressions such as (
76
) is, however, hindered by several disadvantages. The obvious one is the impossibility of determining all free parameters. For the
N+\pi\to N+\pi
process, simpler expressions are obtained by defining alternative coefficients expressed in terms of linear combinations of the
g_{r}^{(m)}
ones. In view of this, Eq. (
76
) can be simply written as

f^{2}k^{0}\delta\mathcal{A}^{(1)}(n+\pi^{+}\to n+\pi^{+})=d_{1}^{(1)}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+e_{1}^{(1)}i(\mathbf{k}\times\mathbf{k}^{\prime})^{3}.

(77)

where the
d_{1}^{(1)}
and
e_{1}^{(1)}
coefficients are easily read off Eq. (
76
).

Thus, the final expressions obtained for first-order SB effects to the scattering amplitudes for the
N+\pi\to N+\pi
process are given by

\displaystyle f^{2}k^{0}\delta\mathbf{A}(p+\pi^{+}\to p+\pi^{+})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(d_{1}^{(1)}+d_{1}^{(8)}+d_{1}^{(10+\overline{10})}+d_{1}^{(27)})\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{k}^{\prime}

(78)

\displaystyle\mbox{}+(e_{1}^{(1)}+e_{1}^{(8)}+e_{1}^{(10+\overline{10})}+e_{1}^{(27)})i(\mathbf{k}\times\mathbf{k}^{\prime})^{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f^{2}k^{0}\delta\mathbf{A}(n+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{-}),

\displaystyle f^{2}k^{0}\delta\mathbf{A}(p+\pi^{-}\to p+\pi^{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(d_{1}^{(1)}+d_{1}^{(8)}-d_{1}^{(10+\overline{10})}-d_{1}^{(27)}+d_{2}^{(8)}+d_{2}^{(27)})\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{k}^{\prime}

(79)

\displaystyle\mbox{}+(e_{1}^{(1)}+e_{1}^{(8)}-e_{1}^{(10+\overline{10})}+e_{2}^{(8)})i(\mathbf{k}\times\mathbf{k}^{\prime})^{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f^{2}k^{0}\delta\mathbf{A}(n+\pi^{+}\to n+\pi^{+}),

\displaystyle f^{2}k^{0}\delta\mathbf{A}(p+\pi^{0}\to p+\pi^{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(2d_{1}^{(1)}+2d_{1}^{(8)}+d_{2}^{(8)}+d_{2}^{(27)})\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{k}^{\prime}

(80)

\displaystyle\mbox{}+\frac{1}{2}(2e_{1}^{(1)}+2e_{1}^{(8)}+e_{1}^{(27)}+e_{2}^{(8)})i(\mathbf{k}\times\mathbf{k}^{\prime})^{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f^{2}k^{0}\delta\mathbf{A}(n+\pi^{0}\to n+\pi^{0}),

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}f^{2}k^{0}\delta\mathbf{A}(p+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2d_{1}^{(10+\overline{10})}+2d_{1}^{(27)}-d_{2}^{(8)}-d_{2}^{(27)})\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{k}^{\prime}

(81)

\displaystyle\mbox{}+(2e_{1}^{(10+\overline{10})}+e_{1}^{(27)}-e_{2}^{(8)})i(\mathbf{k}\times\mathbf{k}^{\prime})^{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}f^{2}k^{0}\delta\mathbf{A}(n+\pi^{+}\to p+\pi^{0}),

which are written in terms of 11 unknown parameters. It should be highlighted that neither flavor
\mathbf{35}+\overline{\mathbf{35}}
nor flavor
\mathbf{64}
representation participates in the final expressions.

Equations (
78
)-(
81
) can be added to their counterparts obtained in the limit of exact flavor
SU(3)
symmetry, Eqs. (
33
)-(
36
) to get the complete expression
(\mathcal{A}+\delta\mathcal{A})_{\mathrm{tree}}
.

IV.2
SB effects to the scattering amplitude from Fig.
1
(c)

The SB effects to the scattering amplitude from Fig.
1
(c) are obtained following the lines of the previous section. In this case,
A_{\mathrm{vertex}}^{ab}
Eq. (
23
) is a spin-zero object and contains two adjoint indices. A straightforward way to obtain the spin-0 operators with three adjoint indices to account for SB is by forming tensor products of
R^{(ij)(abcd)}
listed in Appendix
C
with
\delta^{ij}
to saturate spin indices. With this procedure, out of the 170 original operators, only 59 remain. There is no need to list those operators. However, after repeating the computation of the action of the flavor projectors on these 59 operators, computing matrix elements and gathering together partial results, only one unknown parameter is required to parametrize SB effects from Fig.
1
(c). The final forms of the amplitudes read

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}+\delta\mathcal{A})_{\mathrm{vertex}}(p+\pi^{+}\to p+\pi^{+})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{4}\frac{k^{0}}{f^{2}}(1-h_{1})

(82)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}+\delta\mathcal{A})_{\mathrm{vertex}}(n+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{-}),

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}+\delta\mathcal{A})_{\mathrm{vertex}}(p+\pi^{-}\to p+\pi^{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{4}\frac{k^{0}}{f^{2}}(1+h_{1})

(83)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}+\delta\mathcal{A})_{\mathrm{vertex}}(n+\pi^{+}\to n+\pi^{+}),

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}+\delta\mathcal{A})_{\mathrm{vertex}}(p+\pi^{0}\to p+\pi^{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{4}\frac{k^{0}}{f^{2}}h_{1}

(84)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}+\delta\mathcal{A})_{\mathrm{vertex}}(n+\pi^{0}\to n+\pi^{0}),

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}+\delta\mathcal{A})_{\mathrm{vertex}}(p+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{2\sqrt{2}}\frac{k^{0}}{f^{2}}

(85)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}+\delta\mathcal{A})_{\mathrm{vertex}}(n+\pi^{+}\to p+\pi^{0}),

where
h_{1}
is the new unknown parameter, which is a linear combination of
\mathbf{1}
,
\mathbf{8}
and
\mathbf{27}
operator coefficients only. Notice that
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{vertex}}(p+\pi^{0}\to p+\pi^{0})
and
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{vertex}}(n+\pi^{0}\to n+\pi^{0})
acquire a non-vanishing value due to SB, whereas
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{vertex}}(p+\pi^{-}\to n+\pi^{0})
and
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{vertex}}(n+\pi^{+}\to p+\pi^{0})
are unchanged. Also notice that the isospin relations (
53
)-(
56
) are unaffected by SB effects, as expected.

V
S
-wave scattering lengths

The results presented so far can be applied to the computation of
s
-wave scattering lengths. The
N\pi
forward scattering amplitude for a nucleon at rest can be readily obtained from Eqs. (
45
) and (
46
) at threshold. Following the lines of Ref.
bernard
, the
s
-wave scattering lengths including the baryon mass splitting and first-order SB can be given by

\displaystyle a^{(1/2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{m_{\pi}}{f^{2}}\left[1+\frac{m_{\pi}}{M_{N}}\right]^{-1}\left[(D+F)^{2}-\frac{4}{9}\left[1+\frac{\Delta}{m_{\pi}}+\frac{\Delta^{2}}{m_{\pi}^{2}}\right]\mathcal{C}^{2}+d_{1}^{(1)}+d_{1}^{(8)}+d_{1}^{(10+\overline{10})}+d_{1}^{(27)}\right]

(86)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a^{+}+2a^{-},

and

\displaystyle a^{(3/2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{m_{\pi}}{f^{2}}\left[1+\frac{m_{\pi}}{M_{N}}\right]^{-1}\left[-\frac{1}{2}(D+F)^{2}+\frac{2}{9}\left[1-\frac{2\Delta}{m_{\pi}}+\frac{\Delta^{2}}{m_{\pi}^{2}}\right]\mathcal{C}^{2}\right.

(87)

\displaystyle\mbox{}+\left.d_{1}^{(1)}+d_{1}^{(8)}-2d_{1}^{(10+\overline{10})}-2d_{1}^{(27)}+\frac{3}{2}d_{2}^{(8)}+\frac{3}{2}d_{2}^{(27)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a^{+}-a^{-},

which of course are valid to order
\mathcal{O}(\Delta^{3}/m_{\pi}^{3})
.

Notice that in the limit
\Delta\to 0
and removing SB effects,

a^{(1/2)}+2a^{(3/2)}=0,

(88)

which is a well-known result obtained in the context of current algebra
wein
;
tomo
. It is important to remark that relation (
88
) is fulfilled even in the presence of the
\mathcal{C}^{2}
term, which accounts for the contribution of decuplet baryons.

The usefulness of Eqs. (
86
) and (
87
) relies entirely on the precise determination of the
SU(3)
invariants
D
,
F
, and
\mathcal{C}
and of course, the six parameters
d_{k}^{(m)}
involved in those equations. The invariants can be extracted from baryon semileptonic decays, for instance. The latter set can be obtained by comparing the theoretical expressions with the available experimental data
part
via a least-squares fit. A detailed analysis requires additional theoretical expressions for which data are available and would involve processes including strangeness. This task implies a non-negligible effort and will be attempted elsewhere.

VI
Concluding remarks

The material exposed in this work represents an enterprising program to understand the baryon-meson scattering processes in the context of the
1/N_{c}
expansion. Specifically, the scattering amplitude for the process
B+\pi^{a}\to B^{\prime}+\pi^{b}
, including the decuplet-octet baryon mass splitting and perturbative
SU(3)
symmetry breaking, has been computed. The formalism is general enough to cover the cases when
B
and
B^{\prime}
are any baryon states and
\pi^{a}
and
\pi^{b}
are any pseudoscalar mesons, provided that the Gell-Mann–Nishijima scheme is fulfilled. In particular, expressions for
N\pi\to N\pi
scattering amplitudes are given here. These amplitudes get simple forms [Eqs. (
39
)-(
42
) and Eqs. (
53
)-(
56
)] once all the ingredients are put together, regardless of breathtaking original expressions, such as (
21
).

One important result extracted from the present analysis is worth mentioning: Once more, it is evident that the spin-1/2 and spin-3/2 baryons are present from the outset because together form an irreducible representation of the spin-flavor symmetry. The inclusion of only the spin-1/2 baryons in the chiral Lagrangian breaks the large-
N_{c}
spin-flavor symmetry explicitly. This is the reason why in HBChPT large cancellations in loop graphs are obtained with intermediate spin-1/2 and spin-3/2 baryons.

To close this paper, it should be mentioned that a comparison of this approach with conventional HBChPT (the effective field theory with no
1/N_{c}
expansion) and other approaches, can not be carried out in full at this moment. Additional expressions for scattering amplitudes should be obtained within the present formalism for other processes for which experimental information in available
part
in the form of scattering lengths, for instance. These processes can include the ones where strangeness is involved. This task will be attempted elsewhere.

Acknowledgements.

The authors are grateful to Consejo Nacional de Ciencia y Tecnología (Mexico) for support through the
Ciencia de Frontera
project CF-2023-I-162.

Appendix A
Reduction of baryon operators

The baryon operators present in the expression for the scattering amplitude (
3
) involve
n
insertions of the baryon mass operator
\mathcal{M}
. Evaluating contributions to all orders is a difficult task. Thus, only the first three terms retained in Eq. (
8
) are evaluated and listed here.

A.1
No insertion of the baryon mass operator

The leading-order term for the scattering amplitude of the process (
3
) is

-\frac{1}{f^{2}k^{0}}k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\langle B^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\pi^{b}(k^{\prime})|[A^{jb},A^{ia}]|B(p)\pi^{a}(k)\rangle,

so the baryon operators to be considered become

[\mathcal{P}^{(1)}+\mathcal{P}^{(8)}+\mathcal{P}^{(27)}]^{abcd}[G^{jd},G^{ic}]=-\frac{1}{2N_{f}}i\delta^{ab}\epsilon^{ijr}J^{r}-\frac{1}{2}id^{abe}\epsilon^{ijr}G^{re},

(89)

[\mathcal{P}^{(1)}+\mathcal{P}^{(8)}+\mathcal{P}^{(27)}]^{abcd}[G^{jd},\mathcal{D}_{2}^{ic}]=-\frac{1}{4}i\epsilon^{ijr}\{G^{ra},T^{b}\}-\frac{1}{4}i\epsilon^{ijr}\{G^{rb},T^{a}\},

(90)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{P}^{(1)}+\mathcal{P}^{(8)}+\mathcal{P}^{(27)}]^{abcd}[\mathcal{D}_{2}^{jd},G^{ic}]=-\frac{1}{4}i\epsilon^{ijr}\{G^{ra},T^{b}\}-\frac{1}{4}i\epsilon^{ijr}\{G^{rb},T^{a}\},

(91)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{P}^{(1)}+\mathcal{P}^{(8)}+\mathcal{P}^{(27)}]^{abcd}[G^{jd},\mathcal{D}_{3}^{ic}]

\displaystyle=

(137)

A.3
Two insertions of the baryon mass operator

Finally, the scattering amplitude contribution with two mass operator insertions in turn has the form

-\frac{1}{f^{2}k^{0}}\left[\frac{\Delta^{2}}{{k^{0}}^{2}}\right]k^{i}{k^{\prime}}^{j}\langle B^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\pi^{b}(k^{\prime})|[A^{jb},[J^{2},[J^{2},A^{ia}]]]|B(p)\pi^{a}(k)\rangle,

(138)

The non-trivial contributions of the triple commutator
[A^{{jb}},[J^{2},[J^{2},A^{{ia}}]]]
to the scattering amplitude are
[A^{{jb}},[J^{2},[J^{2},G^{{ia}}]]]
and
[A^{{jb}},[J^{2},[J^{2},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}]]]
. Thus, for
N_{c}=3
,

\displaystyle[A^{{jb}},[J^{2},[J^{2},A^{{ia}}]]]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}^{2}[G^{{jb}},[J^{2},[J^{2},G^{{ia}}]]]+\frac{a_{1}b_{2}}{N_{c}}[D_{2}^{{jb}},[J^{2},[J^{2},G^{{ia}}]]]+\frac{a_{1}b_{3}}{N_{c}^{2}}[\mathcal{D}_{3}^{{jb}},[J^{2},[J^{2},G^{{ia}}]]]

(139)

\displaystyle\mbox{}+\frac{a_{1}c_{3}}{N_{c}^{2}}[\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{jb}},[J^{2},[J^{2},G^{{ia}}]]]+\frac{a_{1}c_{3}}{N_{c}^{2}}[G^{{jb}},[J^{2},[J^{2},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}]]]+\frac{b_{2}c_{3}}{N_{c}^{3}}[D_{2}^{{jb}},[J^{2},[J^{2},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}]]]

\displaystyle\mbox{}+\frac{b_{3}c_{3}}{N_{c}^{4}}[\mathcal{D}_{3}^{{jb}},[J^{2},[J^{2},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}]]]+\frac{c_{3}^{2}}{N_{c}^{4}}[\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{jb}},[J^{2},[J^{2},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}]]].

The use of the identities

\displaystyle[J^{2},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{jb}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[J^{2},\{J^{2},G^{jb}\}]

(140)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{J^{2},[J^{2},G^{jb}]\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[\{J^{2},J^{2}\},G^{jb}],

[J^{2},[J^{2},G^{ia}]]=2\mathcal{O}_{3}^{ia},

(141)

and

\displaystyle[J^{2},[J^{2},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{ia}]]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[J^{2},[J^{2},\{J^{2},G^{ia}\}]]

(142)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[J^{2},\{J^{2},[J^{2},G^{ia}\}]]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{J^{2},[J^{2},[J^{2},G^{ia}]]\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\{J^{2},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{ia}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\mathcal{O}_{5}^{ia},

yields

\displaystyle[J^{2},[J^{2},A^{ia}]]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}[J^{2},[J^{2},G^{ia}]]+\frac{1}{N_{c}}c_{3}[J^{2},[J^{2},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{ia}]]

(143)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2a_{1}\mathcal{O}_{3}^{ia}+\frac{2}{N_{c}^{2}}c_{3}\mathcal{O}_{5}^{ia}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[A^{{jb}},[J^{2},[J^{2},A^{{ia}}]]]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{1}^{2}[G^{{jb}},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}]+\frac{a_{1}b_{2}}{N_{c}}[D_{2}^{{jb}},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}]+\frac{a_{1}b_{3}}{N_{c}^{2}}[\mathcal{D}_{3}^{{jb}},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}]+\frac{a_{1}c_{3}}{N_{c}^{2}}[\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{jb}},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}]

(144)

\displaystyle\mbox{}+\frac{a_{1}c_{3}}{N_{c}^{2}}[G^{{jb}},\mathcal{O}_{5}^{{ia}}]+\frac{b_{2}c_{3}}{N_{c}^{3}}[D_{2}^{{jb}},\mathcal{O}_{5}^{{ia}}]+\frac{b_{3}c_{3}}{N_{c}^{4}}[\mathcal{D}_{3}^{{jb}},\mathcal{O}_{5}^{{ia}}]+\frac{c_{3}^{2}}{N_{c}^{4}}[\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{jb}},\mathcal{O}_{5}^{ia}].

The first four summands in Eq. (
144
) have already been computed and are listed in Sec.
A.1
. For the remaining four ones, the following relation can be useful,

[X,\{J^{2},Y\}]=\{J^{2},[X,Y]\}-\{Y,[J^{2},X]\}.

(145)

Thus

[G^{{jb}},\{J^{2},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}\}]=\{J^{2},[G^{{jb}},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}]\}-\{\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}},[J^{2},G^{{jb}}]\},

(146)

[D_{2}^{{jb}},\{J^{2},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}\}]=\{J^{2},[D_{2}^{{jb}},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}]\},

(147)

[\mathcal{D}_{3}^{{jb}},\{J^{2},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}\}]=\{J^{2},[\mathcal{D}_{3}^{{jb}},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}]\},

(148)

[\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{jb}},\{J^{2},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}\}]=\{J^{2},[\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{jb}},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}}]\}-\{\mathcal{O}_{3}^{{ia}},\{J^{2},[J^{2},G^{{jb}}]\}\}.

(149)

Once the corresponding flavor projection operators are taken into account, expressions (
147
) and (
148
) are straightforwardly obtained from Eqs. (
99
) and (
A.1
) or Eqs. (
115
) and (
118
), as the case may be. The second summands of Eqs. (
146
) and (
149
) yield

\displaystyle[\mathcal{P}^{(1)}+\mathcal{P}^{(8)}+\mathcal{P}^{(27)}]^{abcd}\{O_{3}^{ic},[J^{2},G^{jd}]\}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 5.69046pt}=id^{abe}\epsilon^{ijr}\mathcal{D}_{3}^{re}+\frac{3}{4}id^{abe}\epsilon^{ijr}\mathcal{O}_{3}^{re}-\frac{1}{2}i\epsilon^{ijm}\{J^{m},\{G^{ra},G^{rb}\}\}+\frac{2}{N_{f}}i\delta^{ab}\epsilon^{ijr}\{J^{2},J^{r}\}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{7}{4}i\epsilon^{imr}\{G^{ja},\{J^{m},G^{rb}\}\}+\frac{1}{4}i\epsilon^{jmr}\{G^{ia},\{J^{m},G^{rb}\}\}+\frac{7}{4}i\epsilon^{imr}\{G^{jb},\{J^{m},G^{ra}\}\}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{1}{4}i\epsilon^{jmr}\{G^{ib},\{J^{m},G^{ra}\}\}-\frac{3}{4}i\epsilon^{ijm}\{G^{ma},\{J^{r},G^{rb}\}\}-\frac{3}{4}i\epsilon^{ijm}\{G^{mb},\{J^{r},G^{ra}\}\}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{1}{8}d^{abe}[J^{2},\{J^{i},G^{je}\}]+\frac{7}{8}d^{abe}[J^{2},\{J^{j},G^{ie}\}]-i\epsilon^{jmr}\{J^{2},\{G^{ia},\{J^{m},G^{rb}\}\}\}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}-i\epsilon^{jmr}\{J^{2},\{G^{ib},\{J^{m},G^{ra}\}\}\}+\frac{1}{2}i\epsilon^{jlm}\{\{J^{i},\{J^{l},G^{ma}\}\},\{J^{r},G^{rb}\}\}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{1}{2}i\epsilon^{jlm}\{\{J^{i},\{J^{l},G^{mb}\}\},\{J^{r},G^{ra}\}\},

(150)

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{1}{16}(20N_{c}-83)d^{abe}[J^{2},\{J^{i},G^{je}\}]-\frac{1}{16}(20N_{c}-51)d^{abe}[J^{2},\{J^{j},G^{ie}\}]+2id^{abe}\epsilon^{ijr}\mathcal{D}_{5}^{re}+\frac{3}{2}id^{abe}\epsilon^{ijr}\mathcal{O}_{5}^{re}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}-\frac{11}{12}\{\mathcal{D}_{2}^{jb},\mathcal{O}_{3}^{ia}\}+\frac{11}{12}\{\mathcal{O}_{3}^{jb},\mathcal{D}_{2}^{ia}\}-\frac{11}{24}\{J^{2},\{T^{a},\{J^{j},G^{ib}\}\}\}+\frac{11}{24}\{J^{2},\{T^{b},\{J^{i},G^{ja}\}\}\}

\displaystyle\mbox{\vrule width=0.0pt\penalty\hskip 17.07182pt}+\frac{4}{N_{f}}i\delta^{ab}\epsilon^{ijr}\{J^{2},\{J^{2},J^{r}\}\}-i\epsilon^{ijm}\{J^{2},\{J^{m},\{G^{ra},G^{rb}\}\}\}+\frac{9}{2}i\epsilon^{imr}\{J^{2},\{G^{ja},\{J^{m},G^{rb}\}\}\}

Appendix C
Operator basis contributing to SB effects

In this section, a list of spin-2 operators containing three adjoint indices is provided. These linearly independent operators are used to build the different
1/N_{c}
expansions responsible for SB to the scattering amplitude. It should be pointed out that there is not a single criterion to rule out anyone of them,
i.e.
, the operator reduction rules described in Ref.
djm95
do not entirely apply in this case so they all participate alike. The operators read

\displaystyle R_{1}^{(ij)(ab8)}

\displaystyle=i\delta^{ij}f^{ab8},

\displaystyle R_{2}^{(ij)(ab8)}

\displaystyle=\delta^{ij}d^{ab8},

The non-trivial matrix elements of these operators are listed in Tables
5
-
24
for the
N\pi\to N\pi
process for completeness. By non-trivial matrix elements it should be understood those matrix elements that either vanish by definition or that result from operators anticommuting with
J^{2}
so they are not listed.
